Definition 13
.

A constrained environment
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}}}
is
valid
if

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}\implies{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}\implies{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{$\Rrightarrow$}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}}}\par({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}})\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}},({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}})\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}=_{\alpha}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}\implies{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}=_{\alpha}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}\par({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}})\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}},({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n^{\prime}}})\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}=_{\alpha}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n^{\prime}}}\implies{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}=_{\alpha}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}y}}

Lemma 6

(Saturated inequivalence)
.

If
\emptyset;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}

with positive atoms only,
then there exists
{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\square}}}}\right]}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}
and a valid
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}
such that

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}\mid\emptyset}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}^{\prime}}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathtt{ne}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}}}\right]}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\right]}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}\right]}}

Let us write
({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}})\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leadsto}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}}}\right]}}
when this
relation holds.

6.3
Focused fun removal

In this section, we describe how to transform any pair of distinct
saturated terms
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}}
in

{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to}}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})}}
into a pair of distinct
saturated terms in the restricted type system

{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{(\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})}}
. We know that we can apply the
neutral model
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}}}}}
to get a pair of terms without
atoms –
{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{(\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to}}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})}}
– and then the
bijections of
Section

2.5
(
2.5
Fun-less types and reification
)
give us terms in

{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{(\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})}}
. But the impact of these transformations
on the focused and saturated term structure must be studied carefully.

Reduction to closed types

When applying the neutral model
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}}}}}
, we turn atomic
types
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+}
into strictly positive types
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+})
of the form

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\dots+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}}}}}
. Consider a binding site of the form

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+})}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}})}}
, which becomes

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\dots+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}}}}})}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}})}}}

after transformation. To recover a valid focused term, we need to
insert a big sum elimination to remove the positive variable
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}

from the context:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{match}~{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}~\mathtt{with}~({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}()}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}/}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}})^{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+})}}}}}}}}

(the family notation here denotes a cascade of sum-eliminations, with
as many cases in total as elements in
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+})
). We
also perform such an elimination on each variable in context at the
root.

The substitution of
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}()}}}}}
for
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+})}}
in
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}

may not create
\beta
-redexes, as a variable
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
of atomic type
may not occur in eliminated-sum position. By inspection of the typing
rules of our focused system, such a variable can only appear in
a judgment of the form
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}}\mathrel{\Uparrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+}
; the focused structure
is preserved by replacing it by the derivation

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}()}}}}}}}}\mathrel{\Uparrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\dots+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}}}}}
.

For a focused term
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
, let us simply write
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}})

for this transformed focused term, splitting over each variable of
type
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+})
.

The
saturated
structure, however, is not preserved by this
change. The problem is that replacing a new variable
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
by
a closed term
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}()}}}}}
may break the condition that only
“new” neutrals are introduced during a saturation phase: any neutral
that would only use
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}
as the new variable introduced by the last
invertible phase is not new anymore.

However, we can show that the
saturated inequivalence
structure
is preserved by this transformation. There is a shared context to
a source of inequality in the transformed terms, where the

let
-bindings might not be new at introduction time, but they
are not redundant, as requested by the inequivalence structure. This
is the property of saturated proofs (besides saturation consistency)
that our distinguishability result relies on.

Theorem 11

(Inequivalence in the model)
.

Suppose we have saturated forms
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}}

with positive atoms only. Let us define

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}})
and

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{1}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}}}}}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}})
.

If
({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{0}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{0}}})\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leadsto}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}_{0}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}_{0}}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}_{0}}}}}}}\right]}}
,
then there exists
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}_{1}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}_{1}}}
,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}_{1}}}
such that

({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{1}}})\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leadsto}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}_{1}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}_{1}}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}_{1}}}}}}}\right]}}

Function elimination

To turn

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}_{1}}}
into terms at function-less types, we use
the transformations presented in
Figure

9
(
9Fun-less focused forms
)
. They
correspond to focused versions of the term transformation

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}\rfloor_{A}}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}\rrfloor_{A}}}
of

Figure

3
(
3Fun-less data types
)
, in two interdependent ways: their
definition assumes the transformed terms respect the focused
structures, which gives us rich information on term shapes, and their
result remain in focused form. The specification of these
transformations is as follows:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}\Rightarrow\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}}\rfloor;\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}}\rfloor\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rfloor_{({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}})}}}}}}:\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}\rfloor\mid\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}}\rfloor\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}};{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}\Rightarrow{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rrfloor_{({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}})}}}}{\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}\rrfloor}{\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}}\rrfloor}\\
\frac{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\hskip 9.24994pt\dots}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}^{\prime}}}}\Rightarrow\exists{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m^{\prime}}},\frac{\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}}\rrfloor\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}\rrfloor\hskip 9.24994pt\dots}{\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}}\rrfloor\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m^{\prime}}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}^{\prime}}}}

The transformation of negative neutrals is more elegantly expressed in
the sequent-calculus syntax of
Curien et al. 2016
: we transform
a command
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}\mid_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}S\rangle:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}}}}
cutting on
a type
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}
into a command

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}\mid_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}S\rangle}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}}\rrfloor\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}}}}
cutting on
\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}\rrfloor
. We will write

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}}\right]}}
when the neutral
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}m}}
has head
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}
– it is of
the form
\langle{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}\mid S\rangle
.

Figure 9:
Fun-less focused forms

\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rfloor_{A}}}\par\text{applies ${{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}\rrfloor_{A}}}$ on all subterms}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\begin{array}[]{l}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{match}~{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}~\mathtt{with}~}}\\
\left|\begin{array}[]{l}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{1}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{2}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{2}\end{array}\right.\end{array}}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{1}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{2}}})}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left(\begin{array}[]{l}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{1}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}}\\
,\\
{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{2}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{2}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}}\\
\end{array}\right)}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{1}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{2}}})}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}})}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{absurd}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}})}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}()}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\text{ impossible}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}()}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}}}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}}}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{1}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{2}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{i}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}/}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}}}}^{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}}}}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}}}}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}}}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{2}\right)}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{2}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\right\rangle^{-}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\right\rangle^{-}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\;({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}};\emptyset\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}}:\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\right\rangle^{-}\mid\emptyset}})}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}\rrfloor{})}}

The definition strongly relies on the inversion of term structure of
focused terms modulo
(\mathord{\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}})
. For example, when we define

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{1}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{2}}})}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}}}}
, we know that

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{1}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{2}}}}}
and can thus assume modulo

(\mathord{\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}})
that
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
is of the form

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{match}~{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}~\mathtt{with}~({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}})^{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}}}}}
. Similarly in the neutral case, we
know that any neutral

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{1}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{2}}})}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}}}
can only appear in
a term applied to an argument
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}}\mathrel{\Uparrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{1}+{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{2}}})}}
–
non-invertible phases are as long as possible, so they cannot stop on
a negative function type – and we know that such an
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}
must be of
the form
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}}
.

The way the focused structure conspires to make this transformation
possible is, in fact, rather miraculous – is it more than just
a hack? In the
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}
case of

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}}}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}M}}}}
, we know that a derivation of
the form
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}}}
can only appear
inside a larger derivation

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}
, which we can replace
by
\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}}\rrfloor\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}}}}\mathrel{\Downarrow}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}i}}}
. In the
neutral case, a
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}n}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}}}}\right\rangle^{+}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathord{\mathunderscore\!\mathunderscore}}}}})}}

can only appear applied to an argument

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}}\mathrel{\Uparrow}\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}}}}\right\rangle^{+}
, but as this is
a shifted negative type we know that
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}
is in fact an invertible
form
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}}};\emptyset\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{1}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}_{2}}}\mid\emptyset
, and
this must be a pair
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{2}\right)}}
, exactly what we needed to define
the transformation. The same phenomenon occurs on the argument

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma_{\mathsf{na}}^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}}\mathrel{\Uparrow}\left\langle{\left\langle{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}\right\rangle^{-}}\right\rangle^{+}
of the double-shifted
case.

Lemma 7

(Function elimination preserves inequivalence)
.

(\exists{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}},\ ({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}})\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leadsto}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}}}\right]}})\\
\implies(\exists{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}},\ ({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rfloor}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}}\rfloor}})\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\leadsto}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}}}\right]}})

(The converse implication also holds, but we don’t need it.)

Lemma 8
.

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rfloor_{A}}}}}\right\rfloor_{\mathtt{foc}}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}~=~{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}\right\rfloor_{\mathtt{foc}}}}}\rfloor_{\left\lfloor A\right\rfloor_{\pm}}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}~=~{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}\right\rfloor_{\mathtt{foc}}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}}\rfloor_{\lfloor{\left\lfloor A\right\rfloor_{\pm}}\rfloor}}}~\in~{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{\left\lfloor A\right\rfloor_{\pm}}\,\right\rrbracket}}

Corollary 3

(Fun-elimination preserves semantic equivalence)
.

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}\par\iff\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rfloor_{A}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\lfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}}\rfloor_{A}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}

6.4
Canonicity

Definition 14
.

Let a
closed substitution

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}
be a mapping from
variables to closed terms of closed types (types without atoms).

We write
{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}}:{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}}
when
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}
is a closed context
and, for any
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:A)}}\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}
we have

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\emptyset\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}}}:A}}
.

If
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}
is a constraint environment, we say that

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}
holds when, for any equation
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}
in

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}
, we have
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{\mathtt{sem}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}
.

We write
{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}}:({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}}})
if

{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}}:{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}}
and
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}
hold.

Theorem 12

(Canonicity)
.

Assume
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}
is consistent,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}
is valid, and

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}^{\prime}}}\mid\emptyset}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathtt{ne}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}}}\right]}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}

is a judgment of closed function-less types. Then there
exists a closed substitution
{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}}:({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi}}}})
such
that
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{sem}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\rho}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}]}}}
.

Corollary 4

(Canonicity)
.

In the sub-system
{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{(\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})}}
:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mid\emptyset}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}^{\prime}}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Xi^{\prime}}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{\mathtt{ne}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\neq{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}}}}}\right]}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}\Rightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}}}\right]}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{ctx}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}D}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}p}}^{\prime}}}}}\right]}}

6.5
Results

Theorem 13

(Saturated terms are canonical)
.

In the system with only positive atoms,
if
\emptyset;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{s\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P}}_{\mathsf{a}}}}

then
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\cancel{\approx}_{\mathtt{ctx}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}
.

Corollary 5

(Contextual equivalence implies equality of saturated forms)
.

If
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathpunct{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}}}:A}}
are (non-focused) terms of
the full simply-typed lambda-calculus
{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to}}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})}}
with

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{\mathtt{ctx}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}
, then for any
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}
with no positive
atoms and
\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}\right\rfloor_{\pm}={{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}},\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\right\rfloor_{\pm}=A
and any
saturated terms

\emptyset;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma_{\mathtt{p}}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash_{s\mathsf{inv}}}}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}\mathpunct{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0},}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}^{\prime}}}}}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N}}\mid\emptyset
such
that
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{{\beta\eta}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}}}\right\rfloor_{\mathtt{foc}}}}
and

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{{\beta\eta}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}^{\prime}}}}}\right\rfloor_{\mathtt{foc}}}}
we have
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\approx_{\mathtt{icc}}}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}u}}^{\prime}}}
.

Corollary 6
.

Contextual and
{\beta\eta}
-equivalence coincide

Corollary 7
.

Equivalence in the full simply-typed
\lambda
-calculus with sums and
the empty type is decidable.

Corollary 8
.

The full simply-typed
\lambda
-calculus with sums and the empty
type has the finite model property.

7
Conclusion

7.1
Other Related Work

Background material

For the reader looking for more historical perspective

Dougherty and Subrahmanyam 2000
gives an interesting, detailed presentation of
the state of the art in separation theorems in absence of sum types,
and of why their approach are difficult to extend to
sums.
Simpson 1995
gives an enjoyable, accessible exposition
of what “completeness” exactly means in the setting of categorical
semantics; in particular, while we can prove that two inequivalent
terms can be distinguished by a choice of finite sets, there is no
fixed choice of finite sets that could separate all pairs of terms. It
also discusses the notion of “typical ambiguity”, and the idea that

{\beta\eta}
-equality is shown to be the maximal consistent relation. At
the time of writing, Simpson’s statement of the 15th

TLCA open problem
is also one
of the clearest expositions of the relation between these questions.

Focusing

The main field of application of focusing to programming is the area
of
logic programming
, where operational semantics correspond to
search strategies, which can often be described as specific choices of
polarization in a focused
logic
[
Chaudhuri et al. 2008b
]
.
Focusing has been used to study programming language principles in

Zeilberger 2009
; when considering non-normal forms (logics with
a strong cut rule) it let us reason finely about evaluation order.

This suggests the general notion of
polarization
, an approach
of programming calculi where compositions (cuts) are
non-associative
[
Curien et al. 2016
]
, modeling effects and
resources. In the present work we consider only focused normal forms
(except for stating the completeness of focusing), which only captures
the pure, strongly normalizing fragment, and thus corresponds to
a
depolarized
system.

Guillaume Munch-Maccagnoni’s work on polarized abstract machine
calculi harps on many ideas close to the present work, in particular

Munch-Maccagnoni and Scherer 2015
. It is conducted in a syntax that is
inspired by the sequent calculus rather than the
\lambda
-calculus;
this choice gives a beautiful dynamic semantics to polarized
systems. In contrast, the focused
\lambda
-calculus as presented is
a system of normal forms, and defining an internal reduction for it
would be awkward. While there is no direct correspondence between
sequent proofs and natural deduction proofs in general, their normal
forms are in one-to-one mapping, so in our context the choice of
abstract machine or
\lambda
-terms-inspired syntax matters less.

Atom-less systems

The structure of normal forms has been studied in

Altenkirch and Uustalu 2004
in the special case of the type system

{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{(\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to}}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},2)}}
, with boolean types instead of general
sums, and no atoms. While conceptually easier to follow than the
Grothendieck logical relations of the more general
normalization-by-evaluation work on sums, they remain challenging in
presence of higher-order functions.

In unpublished work,
Ahmad et al. 2010
work with the type system

{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{(\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to}}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\times}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}})}}
: no atoms, but everything
else. They use focusing as a guiding principle to generalize the
normal forms of
{\mathsf{\Lambda{}C}{(\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\to}}},\mathord{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+}}},2)}}
to everything else – to
our knowledge this is the first work to use focusing to approach sum
types. The result obtained, namely decidability of observational
equivalence in this system, is not striking on its own: in absence of
atoms, all types are finitely inhabited, so two functions can be
compared by testing them on all their input domain. But it is
conducted in a rigorous, inspiring way that shows the promises of the
focused structure. Our own proof of distinguishability of distinct
normal forms is not as elegant as this development, as it uses the
inelegant shortcut of function type elimination. We are convinced that
there exists a beautiful proof that weaves the distinguishability
structure through higher-order abstractions in their style, but have
yet to find it.

7.2
Future Work

Direct distinguishability proof

The use of the positive atoms simplification and the detour through
types without functions are a bit disappointing. There should be
a proof in which all steps are as general and general as possible:
completeness of focusing in the explicitly polarized system,
completeness of saturated forms, and then canonicity of saturated
forms at all types.

Categorical semantics

We wonder what is the relation between the saturated structure and the
existing work on categorical semantics of the
\lambda
-calculus with
finite sums.

Direct comparison algorithm

We prove decidability by reducing equivalence of arbitrary

\lambda
-terms to equivalence of saturated forms. This algorithm can
be implemented, but we would rather use an algorithm that does not
need to compute full saturated normal forms before returning
a result – in particular on inequivalent inputs.

It is reasonably easy to formulate an equivalence algorithm on the
focused forms directly, that would perform the saturation “on the
fly”: at the beginning of each saturation phase, look for all the
neutrals that can be defined in the current context, recursively
compute their equivalence classes, and replace each class by
a distinct free variable – then continue on the neutral
structure. Proving this algorithm correct, however, turns out to be
challenging.

Finally, it would be interesting to have an algorithm expressed
directly on the non-focused terms, that would perform a focusing
transformation on the fly, as much as each step of equivalence
requires. The difficulty then is that neutral subterms are not as long
as possible (they may be interrupted by commuting conversions), so it
is possible to look for neutrals sub-terms definable in the current
context and miss some of them – future reasoning stages may un-stuck
sum-eliminations that in turn un-block neutrals that should have been
bound now. For the type system with non-empty sums, control operators
have been used
[
Balat et al. 2004
]
to solve that issue; can the
technique be extended?

\acks

Amal Ahmed made this work possible by giving the time, motivation
and advice that let it foster. Andrew Pitts gave warm encouragements
to look at the specific question of the empty type, and Alex Simpson
enthusiastically sat through an intense whiteboard session at
Schloss Dagstuhl and provided the final motivation to get it
done. Max New, Didier Rémy and the anonymous referees provided very
useful feedback on the article.

This research was supported in part by the National Science
Foundation (grant CCF-1422133).

We define the following naive semantics for term formers:

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathtt{var}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:A}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{var}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}G({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}})\par\\
\\
\mathtt{pair}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A_{1}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A_{2}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\times A_{2}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{pair}(f_{1},f_{2})(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}(f_{1}(G),f_{2}(G))\par\\
\\
\mathtt{proj}_{i}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\times A_{2}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A_{i}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{proj}_{i}(f)(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}v_{i}\\
\text{where~}f(G)=(v_{1},v_{2})\par\\
\\
\mathtt{lam}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:A}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}B}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A\to B}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{lam}(f)(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}(v\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{A}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}})\mapsto f(G,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\mapsto v)\par\\
\\
\mathtt{app}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A\to B}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}B}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{app}(f,g)(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}f(G)(g(G))\par\\
\\
\mathtt{unit}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{unit}(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}\star\par\\
\\
\mathtt{inj}_{i}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A_{i}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}+A_{2}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{inj}_{i}(f)(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}(i,f(G))\par\\
\\
\mathtt{match}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}+A_{2}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:A_{1}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}B}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\times{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}}:A_{2}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}B}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}B}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{match}(f,g_{1},g_{2})(G)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}g_{i}(G,{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}\mapsto v)\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\text{where~}f(G)=(i,v)\par\\
\\
\mathtt{absurd}:{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}\emptyset}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\\
\mathtt{absurd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}\emptyset\end{array}

By composing together the semantics of the term formers in the
obvious way, we obtain semantics for terms
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}
and one-hole
contexts
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}}
:

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}:A}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\par{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}C}}}}\left[{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\square}}}}:A^{\prime}}}}}\right]}}}}:A^{\prime}}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\in{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}^{\prime}}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A^{\prime}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}}\mathrel{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\vdash}}}A}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}

For example we have

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{2}\right)}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}=\mathtt{pair}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{2}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}})

and

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\square}}\right)}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}(f)=\mathtt{pair}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}_{1}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}},f)
.
In particular,
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left\llbracket{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\square}}}\,\right\rrbracket}}_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}}}}
is the identity function.

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\times A_{2}}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\to\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}\rrfloor}}}}}\;({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{1}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}})}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}}}\;({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{2}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}})}}}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}+A_{2}}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{match}~{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}~\mathtt{with}~\left|\begin{array}[]{l}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{1}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{1}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{1}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\to B}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{1}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\sigma_{2}\;{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{2}}}}\to{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\pi_{2}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}}}\;{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}_{2}}}\end{array}\right.}}}}}}}\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}t}}}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}{\lambda}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}}}.\,{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\mathtt{absurd}({{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}x}})}}}}}\end{array}

\begin{array}[]{l}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{v}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\times A_{2}}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{w_{1}}\mapsto{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{w_{2}}\mapsto{{v}({{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(w_{1},w_{2}\right)}}})}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}}}}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\to\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}\rrfloor}}}}}\par\par\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{v}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}1}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{v}({\star})\par\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{v}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}+A_{2}}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{w}\mapsto{{v}({((1,v))})}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\to B}}}}},{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{{w}\mapsto{{v}({((2,v))})}}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}}}\right)}}\par\\
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llfloor{v}\rrfloor_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}\star\par\\[9.24994pt]
{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{v}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\times A_{2}}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left(w_{1},w_{2}\right)}}}\mapsto{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{v}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{1}\to\llfloor{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}\rrfloor}}}}}}({w_{1}})}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}A_{2}\to B}}}}}}({w_{2}})}\\

{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\llceil{v}\rrceil_{{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}0}}\to B}}}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}\emptyset\end{array}

Definition

(
Depolarization
)
.

\begin{array}[]{l@{\quad\eqdef\quad}l}\left\lfloor{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}^{+}\right\rfloor_{\pm}\hfil\hskip 9.24994pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathsf{def}}}{{=}}\hskip 9.24994pt&{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}X}}\\
